Overview\" href=\"#1-overview\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eIntroduction\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-introduction\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Introduction\" href=\"#introduction\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe Dynamic Analog Simulation Engine (DASE) is a high-performance computing platform designed for the simulation and analysis of Spatially-Aware Temporal Physics (SATP). It provides a powerful, scriptable environment for researchers to explore complex physical phenomena through two distinct, specialized computational engines.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe system is primarily operated via a headless command-line interface (\u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e), which accepts newline-delimited JSON commands. This design facilitates easy integration with automated scripts, AI agents, and other external tools for running complex simulation \"missions.\"\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eDual-Engine Architecture\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-dual-engine-architecture\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Dual-Engine Architecture\" href=\"#dual-engine-architecture\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eDASE employs a dual-engine architecture to meet diverse computational needs:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePhase 4B Analog Engine\u003c/strong\u003e: This is a real-valued, AVX2-optimized engine designed for maximum computational speed (benchmarked at ~2.87 ns/op). It is implemented as a dynamically loaded library (\u003ccode\u003edase_engine_phase4b.dll\u003c/code\u003e) and is ideal for tasks requiring high-throughput analog signal processing and simulation where quantum effects are not the primary focus.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eIGSOA Complex Engine\u003c/strong\u003e: This is a complex-valued, quantum-inspired engine specifically designed for high-fidelity SATP physics simulations. It correctly models non-local causal interactions up to a configurable radius (\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e), a critical feature for accurate physics modeling. Unlike the analog engine, the IGSOA engine is a header-only library compiled directly into the main CLI executable, ensuring tight integration and performance. It exists in 1D, 2D, and 3D variants to support different simulation dimensionalities.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eCore SATP Concepts\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-core-satp-concepts\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Core SATP Concepts\" href=\"#core-satp-concepts\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe IGSOA engine simulates the evolution of a quantum state (Ψ) and its interaction with a realized field (Φ). A key parameter in these simulations is the \u003cstrong\u003enon-local causal radius (\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e)\u003c/strong\u003e, which defines the extent of influence between different points in the simulated space. Recent critical bug fixes have ensured this parameter is correctly implemented, allowing the engine to move beyond simple nearest-neighbor interactions to true non-local physics. The engine supports advanced state manipulation techniques, such as blending external states into the simulation, enabling sophisticated \"SATP v2\" mission profiles.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2. involves scanning different E-field initial conditions and extracting the final state for each run.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eStep 1: Prepare the Mission File\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-step-1-prepare-the-mission-file\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Step 1: Prepare the Mission File\" href=\"#step-1-prepare-the-mission-file\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA mission is a sequence of newline-delimited JSON commands that instruct the DASE CLI. For this guide, we will use \u003ccode\u003emissions/SATP_v2_1D_E-field_scan.json\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis mission file performs the following actions:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCreates\u003c/strong\u003e a 1D IGSOA Complex engine.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eLoops\u003c/strong\u003e five times through the following sequence:\na. \u003cstrong\u003eSets\u003c/strong\u003e the initial state of the engine using \u003ccode\u003eset_igsoa_state\u003c/code\u003e, loading a new E-field value for the quantum state (Ψ) in each iteration.\nb. \u003cstrong\u003eRuns\u003c/strong\u003e the simulation for 10.0 seconds with a non-local causal radius (\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e) of 1.0.\nc. \u003cstrong\u003eExtracts\u003c/strong\u003e the final engine state using \u003ccode\u003eget_state\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDestroys\u003c/strong\u003e the engine to clean up resources.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHere is a snippet of the commands used in the mission:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-json notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"{ \u0026quot;command\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;create_engine\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_type\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_complex\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_id\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_1d_scan\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;grid_size\u0026quot;: 1024, \u0026quot;dimensions\u0026quot;: 1 }\n{ \u0026quot;command\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;set_igsoa_state\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_id\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_1d_scan\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;mode\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;overwrite\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;psi_real_file\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;initial_conditions/psi_1d_E_0.1.bin\u0026quot; }\n{ \u0026quot;command\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;run_simulation\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_id\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_1d_scan\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;duration\u0026quot;: 10.0, \u0026quot;R_c\u0026quot;: 1.0 }\n{ \u0026quot;command\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;get_state\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_id\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_1d_scan\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;format\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;json_compact_verbose\u0026quot; }\n...\n{ \u0026quot;command\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;destroy_engine\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;engine_id\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;igsoa_1d_scan\u0026quot; }\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e{ \u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eigsoa_complex\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003e\"engine_id\"\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003e\"grid_size\"\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003e1024\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003e\"dimensions\"\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003edestroy_engine\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003e\"engine_id\"\u003c/span\u003e: command prompt in the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli\u003c/code\u003e directory and pipe the mission file into the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e executable. Redirect the output to a file for later analysis.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"# From the F:\\satp\\igsoa-sim\\dase_cli directory\ntype ..\\missions\\SATP_v2_1D_E-field_scan.json | dase_cli.exe \u0026gt; ..\\results\\e_field_scan_results.json\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e From the F:\\satp\\igsoa-sim\\dase_cli directory\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003etype\u003c/span\u003e ..\u003cspan class=\"pl-cce\"\u003e\\m\u003c/span\u003eissions\u003cspan class=\"pl-cce\"\u003e\\S\u003c/span\u003eATP_v2_1D_E-field_scan.json \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e|\u003c/span\u003e dase_cli.exe \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e ..\u003cspan class=\"pl-cce\"\u003e\\r\u003c/span\u003eesults\u003cspan class=\"pl-cce\"\u003e\\e\u003c/span\u003e_field_scan_results.json\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cem\u003e(Note: The \u003ccode\u003erun_mission.bat\u003c/code\u003e script provides a convenient wrapper for this command.)\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe CLI will process each JSON command in the mission file sequentially and print the JSON results to \u003ccode\u003estdout\u003c/code\u003e, which are then captured in \u003ccode\u003ee_field_scan_results.json\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eStep 3: Analyze the Results\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-step-3-analyze-the-results\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Step 3: Analyze the Results\" href=\"#step-3-analyze-the-results\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe output file contains a series of JSON objects, including the detailed final state from each \u003ccode\u003eget_state\u003c/code\u003e command. This state information can be used for physics analysis.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe \u003ccode\u003edase_cli\u003c/code\u003e directory includes a Python script, \u003ccode\u003eanalyze_igsoa_state.py\u003c/code\u003e, to perform spectral analysis (FFT) on the extracted state data. To run it, you would typically parse the JSON output to feed the state data into the script.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhile the automation of this analysis is outside the scope of this quick start, the \u003ccode\u003eanalyze_igsoa_state.py\u003c/code\u003e script is the primary tool for validating the physical realism of the simulation output.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3. Command and API Reference\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-3-command-and-api-reference\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3. Command and API Reference\" href=\"#3-command-and-api-reference\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis section provides a detailed reference for the commands accepted by the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e interface and a brief overview of the available APIs.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3.1. JSON CLI Commands\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-31-json-cli-commands\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3.1. JSON CLI Commands\" href=\"#31-json-cli-commands\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe CLI accepts a newline-delimited stream of JSON objects. Each object represents a command to be executed.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecreate_engine\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-create_engine\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: create_engine\" href=\"#create_engine\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eCreates and initializes a new simulation engine instance.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eParameters\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecommand\u003c/code\u003e (string): Must be \u003ccode\u003e\"create_engine\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eengine_type\u003c/code\u003e (string): The type of engine to create. Can be \u003ccode\u003e\"igsoa_complex\"\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003e\"phase4b\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eengine_id\u003c/code\u003e (string): A unique identifier for the new engine instance.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003egrid_size\u003c/code\u003e (integer): The size of the simulation grid (e.g., 1024). Required for \u003ccode\u003eigsoa_complex\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003edimensions\u003c/code\u003e (integer): The number of spatial dimensions (1, 2, or 3). Required for \u003ccode\u003eigsoa_complex\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eExample\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eParameters\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecommand\u003c/code\u003e (string): Must be \u003ccode\u003e\"run_simulation\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eengine_id\u003c/code\u003e (string): The ID of the engine to run.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eduration\u003c/code\u003e (float): The simulation time in seconds.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e (float): The non-local causal radius. This is a critical physics parameter for the \u003ccode\u003eigsoa_complex\u003c/code\u003e engine.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003estatus_interval\u003c/code\u003e (float, optional): The interval in seconds at which to output status updates.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eExample\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight dir=\"auto\"\u003eSets the state of the IGSOA engine's simulation fields (Ψ and Φ). This is a powerful command with multiple modes for advanced state manipulation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eParameters\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecommand\u003c/code\u003e (string): Must be \u003ccode\u003e\"set_igsoa_state\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eengine_id\u003c/code\u003e (string): The ID of the \u003ccode\u003eigsoa_complex\u003c/code\u003e engine to modify.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003emode\u003c/code\u003e (string): The mode of operation. See Section 3.2 for details. Can be \u003ccode\u003e\"overwrite\"\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003e\"add\"\u003c/code\u003e, or \u003ccode\u003e\"blend\"\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ephi_real_file\u003c/code\u003e / \u003ccode\u003ephi_imag_file\u003c/code\u003e (string, optional): Path to a binary file containing the real/imaginary part of the Φ field.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003epsi_real_file\u003c/code\u003e / \u003ccode\u003epsi_imag_file\u003c/code\u003e (string, optional): Path to a binary file containing the real/imaginary part of the Ψ field.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eblend_weight\u003c/code\u003e (float, optional): Required for \u003ccode\u003eblend\u003c/code\u003e mode. A value from 0.0 to 1.0.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eExample (\u003ccode\u003eoverwrite\u003c/code\u003e mode)\u003c/strong\u003e:\n\u003cdiv class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003edestroy_engine\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003e\"engine_id\"\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003emy_sim_1\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e }\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3.2. \u003ccode\u003eset_igsoa_state\u003c/code\u003e Modes\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-32-set_igsoa_state-modes\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3.2. set_igsoa_state Modes\" href=\"#32-set_igsoa_state-modes\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe \u003ccode\u003emode\u003c/code\u003e parameter in the \u003ccode\u003eset_igsoa_state\u003c/code\u003e command allows for fine-grained control over how initial conditions are applied.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003eoverwrite\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e: This mode completely replaces the engine's current state with the data provided in the file(s). This is the most common mode for starting a simulation from a known initial condition.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003eadd\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e: This mode performs an element-wise addition of the state data from the file(s) to the engine's existing state. This can be used to introduce perturbations or superimpose fields.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003eblend\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e: This mode performs a weighted interpolation between the engine's existing state and the new state from the file(s). It requires the \u003ccode\u003eblend_weight\u003c/code\u003e parameter. The new state is calculated as: \u003ccode\u003e(1 - w) * current_state + w * new_state\u003c/code\u003e, where \u003ccode\u003ew\u003c/code\u003e is the \u003ccode\u003eblend_weight\u003c/code\u003e. This is used in \"SATP v2\" missions to smoothly introduce changes over time.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3.3. C++/Python APIs\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-33-cpython-apis\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3.3. C++/Python APIs\" href=\"#33-cpython-apis\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhile the JSON CLI is the primary interface, DASE also exposes underlying APIs:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eC++ API\u003c/strong\u003e: The core engine logic is written in C++. The header files (\u003ccode\u003e.h\u003c/code\u003e) in \u003ccode\u003esrc/cpp\u003c/code\u003e define the classes and functions that can be used to directly embed the engine into other C++ applications. This is the most performant way to use the engine but requires manual integration.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePython Bindings\u003c/strong\u003e: A Python wrapper (\u003ccode\u003edase_engine.pyd\u003c/code\u003e) is provided, which exposes the C++ engine functionality to Python. This is used by some of the analysis scripts and the WebSocket bridge server. It offers a balance between performance and ease of use.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4. Analysis \u0026amp; Validation\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-4-analysis--validation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4. Analysis \u0026amp; Validation\" href=\"#4-analysis--validation\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOnce a simulation is complete, the primary goal is to analyze the output to extract meaningful data and validate the physical realism of the results. The \u003ccode\u003eget_state\u003c/code\u003e command provides the raw data, and a suite of Python scripts is available for processing it.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4.1. Key Analysis Tools\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-41-key-analysis-tools\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4.1. Key Analysis Tools\" href=\"#41-key-analysis-tools\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe main analysis scripts are located in the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli\u003c/code\u003e directory. They are designed to operate on the JSON output produced by the CLI.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eanalyze_igsoa_state.py\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-analyze_igsoa_statepy\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: analyze_igsoa_state.py\" href=\"#analyze_igsoa_statepy\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis is the primary tool for physics validation. It parses the base64-encoded state data from a \u003ccode\u003eget_state\u003c/code\u003e command's output, performs a Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) on the spatial data of the Ψ and Φ fields, and plots the resulting power spectrum.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePurpose\u003c/strong\u003e: To analyze the spectral characteristics of the simulation state. This is crucial for identifying dominant wave modes, verifying expected physical behavior, and detecting numerical instability.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eUsage\u003c/strong\u003e: The script must be modified to parse the specific JSON output file from your simulation. It then extracts the state data, decodes it, and generates plots of the power spectrum vs. wavenumber.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eextract_drift.py\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-extract_driftpy\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: extract_drift.py\" href=\"#extract_driftpy\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis script is used to measure the total energy of the simulation state over time. By comparing the energy at the beginning and end of a run, it can quantify the numerical drift of the simulation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePurpose\u003c/strong\u003e: To verify the energy conservation of the simulation, a key indicator of numerical stability.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eUsage\u003c/strong\u003e: This script typically requires a mission that captures the state at multiple points in time. It then calculates and plots the total energy for each snapshot. For a stable system, this value should remain nearly constant.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4.2. Core Validation Checks\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-42-core-validation-checks\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4.2. Core Validation Checks\" href=\"#42-core-validation-checks\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eValidation involves confirming that the simulation behaves according to the principles of SATP theory. Based on recent development and bug fixes, the following are critical validation checks:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e Performance Scaling\u003c/strong\u003e: A fundamental validation of the IGSOA engine is to confirm that the computational cost of a simulation scales correctly with the non-local causal radius (\u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e). As established in the \u003ccode\u003eRC_FIX_FINAL_REPORT.md\u003c/code\u003e, performance should decrease as \u003ccode\u003eR_c\u003c/code\u003e increases, reflecting the greater number of interactions being calculated. This confirms the critical non-local physics fix is active.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eZero-Drift in Null Field\u003c/strong\u003e: When initialized with a zero-field state (or other stable configurations), the simulation should exhibit near-zero energy drift over time. The \u003ccode\u003eextract_drift.py\u003c/code\u003e tool is used to verify this property.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSpectral Purity\u003c/strong\u003e: For simulations initialized with a single, pure wave mode, the power spectrum generated by \u003ccode\u003eanalyze_igsoa_state.py\u003c/code\u003e should show a single, sharp peak at the corresponding wavenumber. The absence of other significant peaks demonstrates that the simulation is not introducing spurious numerical artifacts.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5. Development \u0026amp; Architecture\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-5-development--architecture\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5. Development \u0026amp; Architecture\" href=\"#5-development--architecture\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis section provides an overview of the project's structure, build system, and software architecture for developers who wish to extend or modify the DASE system.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5.1. Project Structure\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-51-project-structure\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5.1. Project Structure\" href=\"#51-project-structure\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe project is organized into the following key directories:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003esrc/\u003c/code\u003e: Contains the core C++ source code for the simulation engines (\u003ccode\u003ecpp/\u003c/code\u003e) and the Python bindings (\u003ccode\u003epython/\u003c/code\u003e).\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003edase_cli/\u003c/code\u003e: Contains the source for the command-line interface, analysis scripts, and example mission commands. This is the primary user-facing component.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003emissions/\u003c/code\u003e: A collection of example and canonical mission files (in NDJSON format) for running various simulations.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003eresults/\u003c/code\u003e: The default output directory for simulation results.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003edocs/\u003c/code\u003e: Contains detailed markdown documentation on various aspects of the project, from theory to implementation.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ebuild/\u003c/code\u003e: The default directory for build artifacts generated by CMake.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5.2. Build System\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-52-build-system\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5.2. Build System\" href=\"#52-build-system\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe project uses a unified \u003cstrong\u003eCMake\u003c/strong\u003e build system, which has replaced the previous collection of disparate \u003ccode\u003e.bat\u003c/code\u003e build scripts. The primary build configuration is defined in the \u003ccode\u003eCMakeLists.txt\u003c/code\u003e file in the project root.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBuilding the C++ CLI\u003c/strong\u003e: To build the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e executable, configure and generate the project with CMake, then use your chosen C++ compiler (e.g., MSVC, GCC). The build process will automatically include the header-only IGSOA engine.\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"# Example build process from root directory\nmkdir build\ncd build\ncmake ..\ncmake --build .\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e Example build process from root directory\u003c/span\u003e\nmkdir build\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003ecd\u003c/span\u003e build\ncmake ..\ncmake --build \u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003e.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBuilding Python Bindings\u003c/strong\u003e: The \u003ccode\u003esetup.py\u003c/code\u003e script in \u003ccode\u003esrc/python\u003c/code\u003e is used to build the \u003ccode\u003edase_engine.pyd\u003c/code\u003e Python module. This process is also integrated into the main CMake build.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5.3. Architecture Overview\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-53-architecture-overview\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5.3. Architecture Overview\" href=\"#53-architecture-overview\"\u003e\u003csvg class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHeadless CLI Core\u003c/strong\u003e: The central architectural principle is the headless \u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e application that processes JSON commands. This ensures maximum flexibility and scriptability.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHeader-Only IGSOA Engine\u003c/strong\u003e: The IGSOA Complex engine is implemented as a header-only library (\u003ccode\u003e.h\u003c/code\u003e files in \u003ccode\u003esrc/cpp\u003c/code\u003e). This means that any changes to the physics or core logic of the IGSOA engine require a full rebuild of the \u003ccode\u003edase_cli.exe\u003c/code\u003e executable that includes it. The Phase 4B engine, by contrast, is a standard dynamic library.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWeb Interface (Future)\u003c/strong\u003e: As detailed in \u003ccode\u003eINTEGRATION_PLAN.md\u003c/code\u003e, a web-based visual interface is planned. The \u003ccode\u003esrc/python/bridge_server_improved.py\u003c/code\u003e provides a WebSocket server that acts as a bridge between a web front-end and the Python bindings. This component is currently in a prototype stage and is not yet fully integrated.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/article\u003e","renderedFileInfo":null,"shortPath":null,"symbolsEnabled":true,"tabSize":4,"topBannersInfo":{"overridingGlobalFundingFile":false,"globalPreferredFundingPath":null,"showInvalidCitationWarning":false,"citationHelpUrl":"https://docs.github.com/github/creating-cloning-and-archiving-repositories/creating-a-repository-on-github/about-citation-files","actionsOnboardingTip":null},"truncated":false,"viewable":true,"workflowRedirectUrl":null,"symbols":{"timed_out":false,"not_analyzed":false,"symbols":[{"name":"DASE Engine Operations Manual","kind":"section_1","ident_start":2,"ident_end":31,"extent_start":0,"extent_end":15823,"fully_qualified_name":"DASE Engine Operations Manual","ident_utf16":{"start":{"line_number":0,"utf16_col":2},"end":{"line_number":0,"utf16_col":31}},"extent_utf16":{"start":{"line_number":0,"utf16_col":0},"end":{"line_number":224,"utf16_col":0}}},{"name":"1.
